This Board of three members sits as a hearing board on appeals presented by state employees in the classified service aggrieved by per- 430

sonnel action resulting in their discharge, demotion, suspension, or lay off. Appeals resulting from alleged racial, political, religious, sex, age and physical handicap discrimination in the state service are also heard by the Board. Membership of the Board by law is politically bipartisian. The Board schedules approximately 150 hearings annually. 

Transcripts of hearings are made available to the courts, Attorney General's Department, attorneys and various departments when required. 

RHODE ISLAND BOARD OF REGISTRATION FOR PROFESSIONAL ENGINEERS AND LAND SURVEYORS DANIEL H. MacLELLAN, Chairman Established 1938—Statutory Authority, Chapter 5-8 

Office- Room 020 State Office Building, Providence 02903 

Full time Employees

Total Expenditures 

$24,222.58

The Board has the responsibility for registering all qualified professional engineers and land surveyors in the state. Presently, 2,386 professional engineers and 251 land surveyors are registered in Rhode Island.

Highlights During the fiscal year the Board held an election and a new chairman and officers were elected to the 5 member board. 

There were nineteen meetings during the fiscal year ending 

June 30, 1977.

The Board was host at a meeting in Rhode Island to the boards of the Northeast Region in February, 1977. The results of the meeting created a much improved liaison and reciprocal relationship between the New England states governing the compliance with engineering examination for registration.

The Board has now instituted a new system utilizing computer programming for the annual license renewals of qualified regis- 431

trants. It is expected this new procedure will improve registration procedures, accounting methods and above all, maintain an annual roster as required by law. 

The commission has instituted an increase in the annual license renewal fees from the $10.00 annual to $20.00, as passed by the 

General Assembly in 1976. This act amends Section 5-8-15 of the general laws of Chapter 5-8; (76 H 7537) “Engineers and Land 

Surveyors". The increase in renewal fees will increase annual income by 100% to the Board and General Treasury, approximate sum of $52,000 annually. • New applicants totalled: professional engineers by examination, 60; professional engineers by comity, 56. Total number of applicants certified as engineers in training examination, 66; land surveyors by examination, 9; land surveyors by comity, 1.

As per direction of the National Council of Engineers Examiners, the Rhode Island Board of Registration undertook the presentation of examination twice a year, April and November.

The National Council of Engineers has adopted a syllabus of examinations in the Fundamentals of Engineering (EIT) and Professional Practice (PE) in the respective disciplines of Mechanical, Electrical, Civil and Chemical. The Rhode Island Board sup ports and adopts the policy of uniformity in examinations among states.

During the year Rhode Island advanced $1,347,850.00 for payment of interest on Newport Bridge Bonds, guaranteed by the state, which due to lack of funds the Authority was unable to pay. When toll revenues are sufficient, the amount will be repaid.

WATER RESOURCES BOARD ALEXANDER A. DiMARTINO, Chairman Established 1955-Statutory Authority, Chapter 46-15

The Water Resources Board provides for the coordinated development, conservation and use of Rhode Island water resources through the formulation and maintenance of a long range, comprehensive plan and implementation program. Currently, this guide plan outlines the present and future developments considered necessary to insure that Rhode Island has an adequate water supply equitably apportioned throughout the state for domestic, industrial and municipal purposes. 

The Water Resources Board is continually assisting state and muni- 433

cipal departments, special water districts, private firms, and other aplicable agencies in achieving a cooperative program for the efficient acquisition, construction, operation and financing of water supply facilities. The Board is authorized, within certain statutory limitations, to acquire sites and construct facilities for water supply, as evidenced by the acquisition of the Big River and Wood River Reser voir sites.

Highlights • At the end of the fiscal year 1977, one land asquisition case remained for settlement or court action. Property management activities involved the leasing to and rent collection from 130 tenants, including both dwellers and businesses. 

The Water Resources Board was a participant in the Governor's Environmental Task Force. This organization's function is to examine the state's entire approach to environmental matters and make recommendations that will give force and focus to efforts to preserve and improve the environment.

The Water Development Fund as of June 30, 1977, consisted of 

$1,124,246 on hand of which the following was on loan: $37,500 to the city of Newport, $199,597 to the city of Woonsocket, $225,000 to the Kent County Water Authority, and $150,000 to the Water Resources Board for preliminary work on the Big 

River Reservoir. • By virtue of the Water Resources Planning Act, established by Congress, the Board received a matching grant from the federal government in the amount of $49,100 for the purpose of preparing a comprehensive plan for water and related land resources in the state. The formulation of the plan has been programmed into seventeen separate tasks.

To date, the tasks completed are as follows: Task #1, Inventory of Water Resources Reports; Task #2, Municipal Water Supplies and Water Use; Task #3, Inventory of Industrial Water Supplies and Water Use; Task #4, The Inventory of Municipal Waste Treatment Plants; Task #6, Rhode Island Basins; Task #8, Rhode Island Lakes and Ponds; Task #13, Inventory of Solid Waste Disposal; Task #10, Character and Analysis of the Fish and Wildlife Inhabiting Rhode Island's Fresh Water Areas. 

Task #7, Inventory and Characteristics of Rhode Island Ground Water Reservoirs; and Task #9, Rhode Island Land Use Analy- 

434

sis were continuing. Task #2, Municipal Water Supplies and 

Task #6, Rhode Island River Basins were being updated. 

The Water Resources Board working within a cooperative agreement with the United States Geological Survey published "Geohydrological Data for the Chipuxet River Ground-Water Reservoir, Rhode Island."

The Water Resources Board has received a total of $478,262 from surplus earth removal activities from the Division Street-Hopkins Hill Road area at the Big River Reservoir site. Of this figure, $6,705 was received in fiscal year 1977. Three companies have contracted for these activities.

The Water Resources Board continued to license well contractors operating in Rhode Island. During the fiscal year about 281 contractor's reports were returned to the Board. These reports give depth and yield of wells and show general areas in Rhode Island where wells are being drilled. 

The ground-water program continued with work in southern Rhode Island in cooperation with the U.S. Geological Survey and the Civil and Environmental Engineering Department at the 

University of Rhode Island through its Water Resources Center. 

Work was concentrated in the lower Wood River ground-water reservoir. Field work involved the drilling of about 85 test wells and pumping tests at 7 proposed pumping centers. Digital and analog models were prepared to assist in future development and operation of the lower Wood River Reservoir. Monitoring of water levels, streamflow, and stream quality were continued in the Beaver, Queen-Usquepaug, and Chipuxet ground-water reservoirs. Preparation of basic data reports continued on the Beaver 

Reservoir. State-wide monitoring of ground-water levels was continued. The Wolf Rock pumping center in Exeter was acquired for future water supply. 435

WORKMEN'S COMPENSATION COMMISSION EUGENE J. LAFERRIERE, Chairman Established 1954–Statutory Authority, Chapter 28-30 

Central Office-25 Canal Street, Providence 02903 

Full-time Employees

Total Expenditures 

$249,770.00

The Commission consists of three members who hear and decide disputes arising between employers and employees in matters of compensation for injury; the reasonableness of medical and hospital bills; and the extent and duration of payments. Highlights There has been a sharp increase in the caseload during the past years. In the fiscal year 1972-73, 1,701 petitions were filed. There were 2,426 petitions filed in the fiscal year ending June 30, 1977 a 70% increase in the number of cases filed since fiscal year 1972-73. 

It is impossible, with the present personnel of this Commission, which has not been increased since the Commission was established in 1954, to comply with Title 28-35-17 of the Workmen's Compen sation Act which requires that a hearing on a petition shall be fixed not later than 30 days next after filing of any petition. 

The Commission is studying the feasibility of adopting new rules and regulations concerning the procedure before the Commission. 

A committee has been appointed to conduct such a study. 

The Rhode Island Workmen's Compensation Act has been amended many times since the last complete revision which was in 1969. Piecemeal amendments have produced inconsistencies and further, due to the increased caseload which will continue to increase, it is felt that a committee should be appointed to study the entire Act and make recommendations to the General Assembly. 

The members of the Commission, individually and collectively, are available at all times for discussion with any member of the General Assembly or any committee thereof, upon any question concerning workmen's compensation. 436
